Then at dinner my wife will make something and I’ll just kind of pick at it, which makes me feel bad because she actually cooks good food.\n\nAnd it sounds small, but my jeans are starting to feel loose, so I figured maybe I should say something instead of just ignoring it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of flat feeling, like the apartment is too quiet. And it’s weird, because usually I can pick up my guitar for a bit and it’ll at least shift my mood some, but lately even that doesn’t really do much.\n\nI don’t know, I keep thinking maybe it’s just the weather or the place being too quiet during the day, but… yeah, I’ve been feeling pretty down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like a plan or anything. Sometimes I get this feeling like everything is just kind of pointless, but then I’ll get distracted by, I don’t know, the sink being full or needing to take the trash out, and I’ll spend twenty minutes reorganizing the recycling because our building has these weird rules about cardboard.\n\nBut hurting myself, no… I don’t think I’ve been there. It’s more like I feel worn out than wanting to do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. Like… enjoyment feels like a weird word right now. I’ll sit down to watch something and then spend ten minutes scrolling through the menu, and then I get distracted because the remote batteries are dying and I start looking for the little screwdriver for the back panel—actually, why do they make those so annoying?\n\nBut, yeah, I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying stuff or just… doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling especially anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, maybe a little? I wouldn’t say it’s been the main thing I notice. I do worry about random stuff, like if I paid a bill or whether I left a dish soaking too long, which sounds stupid when I say it out loud.\n\nBut “especially anxious” is hard to answer. Sometimes I can’t tell if I’m worried or just… sitting there thinking in circles. Like yesterday I spent way too long trying to remember where I put this one receipt, and then I ended up cleaning out a drawer instead, so I’m not sure that counts.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life shortly before this started?\n\nPatient: Maybe? I mean, not like one big dramatic thing happened. It’s been about… three weeks, I think, this low stretch.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule. Which sounds normal, I know—people work full-time all the time—but the house rhythm changed, and I guess I didn’t really notice it at first. I kept thinking I’d adjust once we figured out groceries and laundry and all that stuff, like we have this weird backlog of mismatched socks that somehow keeps growing… but yeah, around then is when it started.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife starting full-time work is the main reason you’ve been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it the main reason. It’s kind of… nearby, if that makes sense? Like, I still do some of the usual stuff, but I don’t always know if I’m enjoying it or just doing it because it’s there.\n\nI tried watching this show I normally like, and then I got distracted noticing how dusty the TV stand was, which made me think about where we put the microfiber cloths… so it’s hard to say whether I lost interest or just didn’t really settle into it. I guess I haven’t been paying close attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been lately?\n\nPatient: It’s… not terrible in the sense that I am sleeping. Not like dramatic slow-motion or anything, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nI’ll have my laptop open and just kind of sit there for ages before I even move my hands to type. Or I’ll realize the keyboard isn’t plugged in, and I know I need to plug it in, but there’s this weird delay before I actually do it.\n\nIt’s annoying because it’s such a small thing, you know? Like the cable is right there. I don’t know if other people would notice, but I notice it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’ll sit down to watch something and then spend ten minutes scrolling through the menu, and then I get distracted because the remote batteries are dying and I start looking for the little screwdriver for the back panel—actually, why do they make those so annoying?\n\nBut, yeah, I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying stuff or just… doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling especially anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, maybe a little? I wouldn’t say it’s been the main thing I notice. I do worry about random stuff, like if I paid a bill or whether I left a dish soaking too long, which sounds stupid when I say it out loud.\n\nBut “especially anxious” is hard to answer. Sometimes I can’t tell if I’m worried or just… sitting there thinking in circles. Like yesterday I spent way too long trying to remember where I put this one receipt, and then I ended up cleaning out a drawer instead, so I’m not sure that counts.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life shortly before this started?\n\nPatient: Maybe? I mean, not like one big dramatic thing happened. It’s been about… three weeks, I think, this low stretch.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule. Which sounds normal, I know—people work full-time all the time—but the house rhythm changed, and I guess I didn’t really notice it at first. I kept thinking I’d adjust once we figured out groceries and laundry and all that stuff, like we have this weird backlog of mismatched socks that somehow keeps growing… but yeah, around then is when it started.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife starting full-time work is the main reason you’ve been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it the main reason. It’s kind of… nearby, if that makes sense? Like it’s one of the things that changed around the same time.\n\nBut I also don’t want to blame her job, because it’s good that she’s working, and she likes parts of it, I think. Or at least she likes the people there—she was telling me about someone who labels their lunch containers with full dates, which honestly seems smart but also intense.\n\nSo… maybe it contributed? I’m not sure it explains everything.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Like, I still do some of the usual stuff, but I don’t always know if I’m enjoying it or just doing it because it’s there.\n\nI tried watching this show I normally like, and then I got distracted noticing how dusty the TV stand was, which made me think about where we put the microfiber cloths… so it’s hard to say whether I lost interest or just didn’t really settle into it. I guess I haven’t been paying close attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been lately?\n\nPatient: It’s… not terrible in the sense that I am sleeping. Like I get to bed and I’m not up all night or anything.\n\nBut it’s been broken up. I keep waking up around four or five, and then my brain starts doing that thing where it’s like, “Oh, let’s think about bills,” or I’ll remember some unfinished music track I meant to work on, and then suddenly I’m mentally rearranging the whole thing instead of sleeping. Which is annoying, because by the time the day actually starts, I already feel tired.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if other people would notice, but I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really because of weight, no. The past few weeks I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then at dinner I’m kind of picking at it even if my wife made something good. Which makes me feel bad, because she’ll ask if it’s okay and I’m like, yeah, it’s fine, I’m just… I don’t know.\n\nAnd I noticed my jeans are starting to feel a little loose, which is actually what made me think maybe it’s enough to mention.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like yesterday I spent way too long trying to remember where I put this one receipt, and then I ended up cleaning out a drawer instead, so I’m not sure that counts.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life shortly before this started?\n\nPatient: Maybe? I mean, not like one big dramatic thing happened. It’s been about… three weeks, I think, this low stretch.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule. Which sounds normal, I know—people work full-time all the time—but the house rhythm changed, and I guess I didn’t really notice it at first. I kept thinking I’d adjust once we figured out groceries and laundry and all that stuff, like we have this weird backlog of mismatched socks that somehow keeps growing… but yeah, around then is when it started.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife starting full-time work is the main reason you’ve been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it the main reason. I do worry about random stuff, like if I paid a bill or whether I left a dish soaking too long, which sounds stupid when I say it out loud.\n\nBut “especially anxious” is hard to answer. Sometimes I can’t tell if I’m worried or just… sitting there thinking in circles. Like yesterday I spent way too long trying to remember where I put this one receipt, and then I ended up cleaning out a drawer instead, so I’m not sure that counts.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life shortly before this started?\n\nPatient: Maybe? I mean, not like one big dramatic thing happened. It’s been about… three weeks, I think, this low stretch.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule. Which sounds normal, I know—people work full-time all the time—but the house rhythm changed, and I guess I didn’t really notice it at first. I kept thinking I’d adjust once we figured out groceries and laundry and all that stuff, like we have this weird backlog of mismatched socks that somehow keeps growing… but yeah, around then is when it started.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife starting full-time work is the main reason you’ve been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it the main reason. It’s kind of… nearby, if that makes sense? I’m not sure it explains everything.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Like, I still do some of the usual stuff, but I don’t always know if I’m enjoying it or just doing it because it’s there.\n\nI tried watching this show I normally like, and then I got distracted noticing how dusty the TV stand was, which made me think about where we put the microfiber cloths… so it’s hard to say whether I lost interest or just didn’t really settle into it. I guess I haven’t been paying close attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been lately?\n\nPatient: It’s… not terrible in the sense that I am sleeping. Like I get to bed and I’m not up all night or anything.\n\nBut it’s been broken up. Which makes me feel bad, because she’ll ask if it’s okay and I’m like, yeah, it’s fine, I’m just… I don’t know.\n\nAnd I noticed my jeans are starting to feel a little loose, which is actually what made me think maybe it’s enough to mention.\n\nDoctor: Have these low feelings been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s been most days. Most days lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, kind of flat feeling, like the apartment is too quiet. And it’s weird, because usually I can pick up my guitar for a bit and it’ll at least shift my mood some, but lately even that doesn’t really do much.\n\nI don’t know, I keep thinking maybe it’s just the weather or the place being too quiet during the day, but… yeah, I’ve been feeling pretty down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living or of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like a plan or anything. Sometimes I get this feeling like everything is just kind of pointless, but then I’ll get distracted by, I don’t know, the sink being full or needing to take the trash out, and I’ll spend twenty minutes reorganizing the recycling because our building has these weird rules about cardboard.\n\nBut hurting myself, no… I don’t think I’ve been there. It’s more like I feel worn out than wanting to do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. Like… enjoyment feels like a weird word right now. I’ll sit down to watch something and then spend ten minutes scrolling through the menu, and then I get distracted because the remote batteries are dying and I start looking for the little screwdriver for the back panel—actually, why do they make those so annoying?\n\nBut, yeah, I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying stuff or just… doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling especially anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, maybe a little? I wouldn’t say it’s been the main thing I notice. I do worry about random stuff, like if I paid a bill or whether I left a dish soaking too long, which sounds stupid when I say it out loud.\n\nBut “especially anxious” is hard to answer. Sometimes I can’t tell if I’m worried or just… sitting there thinking in circles. Like yesterday I spent way too long trying to remember where I put this one receipt, and then I ended up cleaning out a drawer instead, so I’m not sure that counts.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life shortly before this started?\n\nPatient: Maybe? I mean, not like one big dramatic thing happened. It’s been about… three weeks, I think, this low stretch.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule. Which sounds normal, I know—people work full-time all the time—but the house rhythm changed, and I guess I didn’t really notice it at first. I kept thinking I’d adjust once we figured out groceries and laundry and all that stuff, like we have this weird backlog of mismatched socks that somehow keeps growing… but yeah, around then is when it started.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife starting full-time work is the main reason you’ve been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it the main reason. It’s kind of… nearby, if that makes sense? Like it’s one of the things that changed around the same time.\n\nBut I also don’t want to blame her job, because it’s good that she’s working, and she likes parts of it, I think. Or at least she likes the people there—she was telling me about someone who labels their lunch containers with full dates, which honestly seems smart but also intense.\n\nSo… maybe it contributed? I’m not sure it explains everything.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Like, I still do some of the usual stuff, but I don’t always know if I’m enjoying it or just doing it because it’s there.\n\nI tried watching this show I normally like, and then I got distracted noticing how dusty the TV stand was, which made me think about where we put the microfiber cloths… so it’s hard to say whether I lost interest or just didn’t really settle into it. I guess I haven’t been paying close attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been lately?\n\nPatient: It’s… not terrible in the sense that I am sleeping. Like I get to bed and I’m not up all night or anything.\n\nBut it’s been broken up. I keep waking up around four or five, and then my brain starts doing that thing where it’s like, “Oh, let’s think about bills,” or I’ll remember some unfinished music track I meant to work on, and then suddenly I’m mentally rearranging the whole thing instead of sleeping. Which is annoying, because by the time the day actually starts, I already feel tired.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like dramatic slow-motion or anything, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nI’ll have my laptop open and just kind of sit there for ages before I even move my hands to type. Or I’ll realize the keyboard isn’t plugged in, and I know I need to plug it in, but there’s this weird delay before I actually do it.\n\nIt’s annoying because it’s such a small thing, you know? Like the cable is right there. I don’t know if other people would notice, but I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really because of weight, no. The past few weeks I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then at dinner I’m kind of picking at it even if my wife made something good. Which makes me feel bad, because she’ll ask if it’s okay and I’m like, yeah, it’s fine, I’m just… I don’t know.\n\nAnd I noticed my jeans are starting to feel a little loose, which is actually what made me think maybe it’s enough to mention.\n\nDoctor: Have these low feelings been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s been most days. It’s hard to pin down exactly because the days kind of blur together, but I’d say this stretch has been about three weeks now.\n\nIt sort of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule. Which sounds weird, like I’m blaming her schedule or something—I’m not—but that’s around when I noticed I was just… lower most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, concentration’s been… not great.\n\nLike if I try to work on code, I’ll read the same line over and over and it just won’t click what I’m supposed to do next. Or with music, I’ll replay the same eight bars of a song, thinking I’m going to fix the part or add something, and then I’m just sitting there looping it and not deciding anything.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because those are usually things I can get absorbed in. Lately it’s more like I’m staring at them than actually doing them.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or guilty?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not in a big, dramatic way, I guess. Like I’m not sitting there thinking I’m worthless over and over.\n\nGuilty is harder to answer, because I’ll feel bad about random stuff, like if I’m not pulling my weight around the house, or if my wife asks me something and I give a short answer. But then I wonder if that’s just normal guilt, you know? Like everybody feels bad when they’re being kind of useless.\n\nSorry, I’m kind of going in circles. I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t feel like I’ve suddenly got no energy at all or anything.

It’s more like… I don’t know what counts as “unusually” low. Some days I’m just kind of slow to get going, but I also have days where I’ve always been like that, even before all this. Yeah… I think so. Most days lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling, like the apartment is too quiet or something.

I’m not sure it explains everything.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Like, I still do some of the usual stuff, but I don’t always know if I’m enjoying it or just doing it because it’s there.\n\nI tried watching this show I normally like, and then I got distracted noticing how dusty the TV stand was, which made me think about where we put the microfiber cloths… so it’s hard to say whether I lost interest or just didn’t really settle into it. I guess I haven’t been paying close attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been lately?\n\nPatient: It’s… not terrible in the sense that I am sleeping. Like I get to bed and I’m not up all night or anything.\n\nBut it’s been broken up. I keep waking up around four or five, and then my brain starts doing that thing where it’s like, “Oh, let’s think about bills,” or I’ll remember some unfinished music track I meant to work on, and then suddenly I’m mentally rearranging the whole thing instead of sleeping. Which is annoying, because by the time the day actually starts, I already feel tired.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like dramatic slow-motion or anything, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nI’ll have my laptop open and just kind of sit there for ages before I even move my hands to type. Like the cable is right there. I don’t know if other people would notice, but I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really because of weight, no. The past few weeks I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then at dinner I’m kind of picking at it even if my wife made something good. Which makes me feel bad, because she’ll ask if it’s okay and I’m like, yeah, it’s fine, I’m just… I don’t know.\n\nAnd I noticed my jeans are starting to feel a little loose, which is actually what made me think maybe it’s enough to mention.\n\nDoctor: Have these low feelings been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s been most days. Lately it’s more like I’m staring at them than actually doing them.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or guilty?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not in a big, dramatic way, I guess. Like I’m not sitting there thinking I’m worthless over and over.\n\nGuilty is harder to answer, because I’ll feel bad about random stuff, like if I’m not pulling my weight around the house, or if my wife asks me something and I give a short answer. But then I wonder if that’s just normal guilt, you know? Like everybody feels bad when they’re being kind of useless.\n\nSorry, I’m kind of going in circles. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear “worthless” feeling. Guilt, maybe a little here and there, but I’m not sure it’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t feel like I’ve suddenly got no energy at all or anything.\n\nIt’s more like… I don’t know what counts as “unusually” low. Some days I’m just kind of slow to get going, but I also have days where I’ve always been like that, even before all this. I used to joke in college that I needed three separate starts to the day before I actually did anything, so it’s hard for me to tell what’s new versus just me being kind of… sluggish sometimes.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t specifically noticed low energy as the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: During these past three weeks, have you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Kind of, yeah. I mean, I’m still doing the basic stuff at home — dishes get done eventually, laundry isn’t like… a disaster. So it’s not like everything has fallen apart.

But the things I was trying to build have sort of stopped. I had a couple music projects I was excited about, and I’d reached out to some musicians, like actually made contact, which is usually the hard part for me. And then I just… didn’t follow through. Or I waited too long to respond, and then it felt awkward, and then I avoided it more.

So day to day, I’m functioning, I guess, but not really moving anything forward.

Not like dramatic slow-motion or anything, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nI’ll have my laptop open and just kind of sit there for ages before I even move my hands to type. Like the cable is right there. I don’t know if other people would notice, but I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really because of weight, no. It’s more like I feel worn out than wanting to do something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I’ll sit down to watch something and then spend ten minutes scrolling through the menu, and then I get distracted because the remote batteries are dying and I start looking for the little screwdriver for the back panel—actually, why do they make those so annoying?\n\nBut, yeah, I guess I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying stuff or just… doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling especially anxious or worried lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… anxious, maybe a little? I wouldn’t say it’s been the main thing I notice. I do worry about random stuff, like if I paid a bill or whether I left a dish soaking too long, which sounds stupid when I say it out loud.\n\nBut “especially anxious” is hard to answer. Sometimes I can’t tell if I’m worried or just… sitting there thinking in circles. Like yesterday I spent way too long trying to remember where I put this one receipt, and then I ended up cleaning out a drawer instead, so I’m not sure that counts.\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life shortly before this started?\n\nPatient: Maybe? I mean, not like one big dramatic thing happened. It’s been about… three weeks, I think, this low stretch.\n\nIt kind of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule. Which sounds normal, I know—people work full-time all the time—but the house rhythm changed, and I guess I didn’t really notice it at first. I kept thinking I’d adjust once we figured out groceries and laundry and all that stuff, like we have this weird backlog of mismatched socks that somehow keeps growing… but yeah, around then is when it started.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your wife starting full-time work is the main reason you’ve been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it the main reason. It’s kind of… nearby, if that makes sense? Like it’s one of the things that changed around the same time.\n\nBut I also don’t want to blame her job, because it’s good that she’s working, and she likes parts of it, I think. Or at least she likes the people there—she was telling me about someone who labels their lunch containers with full dates, which honestly seems smart but also intense.\n\nSo… maybe it contributed? I’m not sure it explains everything.\n\nDoctor: In the past few weeks, have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. Like, I still do some of the usual stuff, but I don’t always know if I’m enjoying it or just doing it because it’s there.\n\nI tried watching this show I normally like, and then I got distracted noticing how dusty the TV stand was, which made me think about where we put the microfiber cloths… so it’s hard to say whether I lost interest or just didn’t really settle into it. I guess I haven’t been paying close attention to that specifically.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been lately?\n\nPatient: It’s… not terrible in the sense that I am sleeping. Like I get to bed and I’m not up all night or anything.\n\nBut it’s been broken up. I keep waking up around four or five, and then my brain starts doing that thing where it’s like, “Oh, let’s think about bills,” or I’ll remember some unfinished music track I meant to work on, and then suddenly I’m mentally rearranging the whole thing instead of sleeping. Which is annoying, because by the time the day actually starts, I already feel tired.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been moving or speaking noticeably more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not like dramatic slow-motion or anything, but everything feels slower right now.\n\nI’ll have my laptop open and just kind of sit there for ages before I even move my hands to type. Or I’ll realize the keyboard isn’t plugged in, and I know I need to plug it in, but there’s this weird delay before I actually do it.\n\nIt’s annoying because it’s such a small thing, you know? Like the cable is right there. I don’t know if other people would notice, but I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding food because you’re worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really because of weight, no. I mean, I’m not sitting there thinking, “Don’t eat, you’ll gain weight,” or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… food just sort of slips past me. The past few weeks I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without really meaning to, and then at dinner I’m kind of picking at it even if my wife made something good. Which makes me feel bad, because she’ll ask if it’s okay and I’m like, yeah, it’s fine, I’m just… I don’t know.\n\nAnd I noticed my jeans are starting to feel a little loose, which is actually what made me think maybe it’s enough to mention.\n\nDoctor: Have these low feelings been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s been most days. It’s hard to pin down exactly because the days kind of blur together, but I’d say this stretch has been about three weeks now.\n\nIt sort of started after my wife settled into her full-time work schedule. Which sounds weird, like I’m blaming her schedule or something—I’m not—but that’s around when I noticed I was just… lower most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, concentration’s been… not great.\n\nLike if I try to work on code, I’ll read the same line over and over and it just won’t click what I’m supposed to do next. Or with music, I’ll replay the same eight bars of a song, thinking I’m going to fix the part or add something, and then I’m just sitting there looping it and not deciding anything.\n\nIt’s frustrating, because those are usually things I can get absorbed in. Lately it’s more like I’m staring at them than actually doing them.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or guilty?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not in a big, dramatic way, I guess. Like I’m not sitting there thinking I’m worthless over and over.\n\nGuilty is harder to answer, because I’ll feel bad about random stuff, like if I’m not pulling my weight around the house, or if my wife asks me something and I give a short answer. But then I wonder if that’s just normal guilt, you know? Like everybody feels bad when they’re being kind of useless.\n\nSorry, I’m kind of going in circles. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear “worthless” feeling. Guilt, maybe a little here and there, but I’m not sure it’s the main thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t feel like I’ve suddenly got no energy at all or anything.\n\nIt’s more like… I don’t know what counts as “unusually” low. Some days I’m just kind of slow to get going, but I also have days where I’ve always been like that, even before all this. I used to joke in college that I needed three separate starts to the day before I actually did anything, so it’s hard for me to tell what’s new versus just me being kind of… sluggish sometimes.\n\nSo I guess I haven’t specifically noticed low energy as the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: During these past three weeks, have you felt sad or empty most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Most days lately I wake up and there’s this heavy, flat feeling, like the apartment is too quiet or something.\n\nIt’s weird because normally if I pick up my guitar, even just messing around, it kind of changes the room for me. Lately I’ll pick it up and it doesn’t really lift anything. I’ll just sit there with it, and then I’m thinking about how the strings need changing, or how I should’ve cleaned up the living room, and then I’m not really playing.\n\nSo… yeah, sad maybe isn’t always the exact word, but empty or flat most of the day, most days, that sounds pretty accurate.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms been making it hard for you to function day to day?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yeah. I mean, I’m still doing the basic stuff at home — dishes get done eventually, laundry isn’t like… a disaster. So it’s not like everything has fallen apart.\n\nBut the things I was trying to build have sort of stopped. I had a couple music projects I was excited about, and I’d reached out to some musicians, like actually made contact, which is usually the hard part for me. And then I just… didn’t follow through. Or I waited too long to respond, and then it felt awkward, and then I avoided it more.\n\nSo day to day, I’m functioning, I guess, but not really moving anything forward.\n\nDoctor: Have you had less appetite than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. That’s kind of one of the things that made me think something was off.\n\nI’ll realize it’s like mid-afternoon and I never ate breakfast or lunch, not really on purpose, just… I don’t get around to it. Then dinner comes and my wife will have made something, and I’ll sort of pick at it even if it’s something I normally like.\n\nI noticed my jeans are feeling looser too, which at first I was like, well, maybe I finally stopped snacking so much, but it doesn’t really feel like that. It’s more like eating just hasn’t been happening normally.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost weight recently?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah. I haven’t weighed myself or anything, but my jeans are starting to feel loose, which is what made me notice it.\n\nIt’s probably tied to the eating thing… like I’ll skip breakfast and lunch without meaning to, then dinner happens and even if my wife cooked something good, I just kind of pick at it. And then I’m thinking, do I need a belt, or did these jeans stretch out in the wash—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, I think I’ve probably lost some weight recently.\n\nDoctor: During these past few weeks, have you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like, I’ll think maybe if I pick up my guitar it’ll shake something loose, because usually that helps, or at least gives me something to focus on. But lately even that doesn’t really lift it. I end up just sitting there holding it, which is… I don’t know, weirdly depressing in itself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
